# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the registry rejects a deployment record that lacks a sidecar root for a set live at registration, and retires a set only when no live entitlement remains under it, so the sets and their liveness are held as records the registry registers, retires, and reads; every contract node of the suite builds, formats, and tests under one toolchain, so the contract that creates the suite creates that toolchain (LC-09 and LC-01; the dependency map's `contracts/evm/ParameterSets` row and its Tools and dependencies decision; the technical requirements' `ParameterSet` record and Registry paragraph, `registerParameterSet` and `retireParameterSet`; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `contracts/evm/ParameterSets`; the tech stack's contract toolchain row; the research notebook's retirement rule, a set retired only when the registry shows no live entitlement under it)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a parameter-set record holds `id`, the parameter-set digest the record is keyed by, `asset`, `suiteId`, `material`, the suite-tagged public parameters as bytes, `identityScope`, a `uint8`, and `live`, a `bool`; the contract stores every member as it is given and interprets none, because suite-specific material and parameter sets are "interpreted only by the delivery verifier the adapter registry binds" (the technical requirements' On-chain state)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: `registerParameterSet` records a set and marks it live; `retireParameterSet` marks a recorded live set not live and keeps every other member of its record; a recorded id, live or retired, is never recorded again, so a retired set's record remains readable (LC-01: the suite represents "parameter sets and their live or retired state")
    * `[✅]`   Functional: only the address that deployed the contract may register or retire a set, the registry that deploys it; whether no live entitlement remains under a set is the registry's decision and is applied before it calls `retireParameterSet`

    * `[✅]`   `test_ParameterSetReturnsAnEmptyRecordForAnUnrecordedId`: contract `parameterSet` of an id no registration recorded returns every member zero and `isParameterSetLive` returns false; assert each member against zero, empty bytes, and false

* `[ ]`   `domain/handshake_key_commitment` **Handshake key commitment, the 32 bytes of the Binding record's `bytes32` commitment to an identity's bound handshake public key together with the scheme identifier the key is bound under, admitted only when the commitment can name a bound key**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: the identity binding stores a commitment to the handshake public key and the scheme identifier the key is bound under, the key itself travelling in the binding transaction's calldata and in the anchored DID Document, so the stored width does not depend on the scheme; the chain family's attempt context carries the identity and this commitment, so a value naming no bound key must be refused before an attempt is built from it (the technical requirements' `Binding` schema, `handshakeKeyCommitment bytes32` and `scheme uint8`; the feature spec's identity binding criterion; the dependency map's `domain/handshake_key_commitment` row and its statement of the chain family's attempt context; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `domain/handshake_key_commitment`)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: one type holds the commitment's 32 bytes and the `SignatureSchemeIdentifier` the key is bound under, each reachable only through a read accessor, and its only producer is a fallible constructor
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the constructor takes exactly 32 bytes, so the length is a fact of the params type and never a runtime check, and takes the scheme as the `SignatureSchemeIdentifier` `domain/signature_scheme_identifier` authors, which is admitted before this constructor runs, so the constructor checks no scheme
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the constructor refuses the all-zero commitment, which is what an unassigned `bytes32` storage slot reads as, so it names no key the Binding record holds
    * `[ ]`   Functional: every other 32-byte value is admitted with the scheme it is handed, and both are read back unchanged
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the module depends on the standard library and on the `signature_scheme_identifier` module alone; the `domain` crate's dependencies are unchanged

  * `[ ]`   `role`
    * `[ ]`   Domain: an owned value type in the protocol and domain ring, the bound key every attempt context and the identity binding name
    * `[ ]`   Does not compute, verify, or derive a commitment, and does not hold the handshake public key; the key travels in calldata and in the DID Document
    * `[ ]`   Does not assign, map, or validate a scheme identifier; `domain/signature_scheme_identifier` owns the type and its refusal
    * `[ ]`   Does not read the Binding record, compare a commitment with a key, or track whether a binding is current; the registry holds the record
    * `[ ]`   Does not decode a commitment from wire bytes of unknown length; the encoding family decodes a `bytes32` into the 32 bytes this constructor takes
    * `[ ]`   Does not create any other module of the `domain` crate
    * `[ ]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[ ]`   `chain/base` **The Base concrete of the chain family, one adapter over `alloy`: Base's settlement tiers and reference age, the registry's three-state authorization view and its batch, a transaction's estimated fee composed from its execution gas and its L1 data fee, and the recovery of a delivered envelope from the settlement events and calldata; binds the forms `chain/create_chain_forms` resolves and authors none; authors the chain family's settlement and entitlement-state interfaces, its attempt context, selection, declared set, consumer surface, description of a fee, and mock, and the language-neutral ABI descriptions of the registry and of the entitlement interface**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: every attempt is gated on chain state read at a declared settlement tier, so the client needs one adapter that maps Base's heads onto the protocol's ordered tier vocabulary, evaluates an attempt context against the registry at one settlement reference, estimates a transaction's fee, and recovers a delivered envelope from chain history; the chain's network values are the adapter's configuration, reached through the family and never held by a consumer (LC-04; LC-06; CD-04; the Concrete Couplings Handoff's problem "the chain's network values" and its decision on the estimated fee; the dependency map's `chain/base` row and `chain/base ownership` list; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `chain/base`; the technical requirements' contract-suite API surface, Database Schemas, and Chain family ownership; the product requirements' resolved position on Base and the contract suite as the on-chain half of the chain family; `docs/research/cryptography.md`, Settlement and the `AttemptContext` paragraph; `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`, Native Credentials and Per-Attempt Authorization; `families.md`, "A family's surface carries no concrete's model")
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the family's settlement interface maps a tier to the most recent reference that has attained it, a reference to how settled it is now, and a reference to its age against the chain's tip; the tiers are `INCLUDED`, `SOFT`, `HARD`, and `SETTLED`, ordered, and the references are opaque and orderable
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the attempt rule reads at the reference `current_reference_at` returns for the deployment's declared tier and destroys at the reference it returns for `HARD`, and the age of a reference is the timestamp of the chain's latest block less the timestamp of the reference's block, so the adapter reads the chain and holds no clock (LC-04: "the references at which the attempt rule reads (that tier) and destroys (`HARD`), and reference age for the `τ_soft` bound")
    * `[ ]`   Functional: Base maps `INCLUDED` to the sequencer's latest head, `SOFT` to the safe head posted to L1, `HARD` to the L1-finalized head, and `SETTLED` to the L2 block height of the anchor state of the OP Stack fault-proof registry read from the L1 node the settings name; a reference whose block the chain no longer holds under the identity the reference recorded is refused as reorganized, and a reference past the latest head is refused as not included
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the family's entitlement-state interface evaluates one attempt context, and a page of contexts, against the registry at the context's settlement reference, returning `AUTHORIZED`, `PENDING_SETTLEMENT`, or `DENIED` for each, the batch returning one result per context in the order given and agreeing with the individual evaluation of each context; a context's every field reaches the call
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the attempt context carries the asset's root, the deployment identity, the entitlement contract and token, the identity and the scheme and commitment of its bound handshake key, the interval its credential was issued for, and the settlement reference of the view, generic over the chain family's forms
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the family's estimated-fee description is a list of entries each carrying a name and a unit from the producing member's declaration and a quantity of the family's own type; Base estimates the fee of the transaction its caller describes, without signing, sending, or settling anything, as the execution gas the endpoint estimates and the L1 data fee the gas price oracle returns for the transaction's unsigned encoding, and refuses an L1 data fee that does not fit the family's quantity, a 128-bit unsigned integer, the execution gas converting to it without loss
    * `[ ]`   Functional: recovery returns, for an entitlement and an interval, the envelope bytes the settlement that created the interval posted, taken from that settlement's event and confirmed against the calldata of the transaction that emitted it, and refuses a disagreement between the two or a settlement the history does not hold
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the family's consumer surface hands the constructed chain to a caller generic over the family's trait, the chain's endpoint, chain identifier, and entitlement contract being read from it through the family's trait
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the adapter reads the endpoint, the settlement endpoint, the chain identifier, the registry its views are called on, the entitlement contract, the gas price oracle, the anchor state registry its `SETTLED` tier reads, and the page limit of its batch view from the settings and the declaration `chain/base/settings` authors, and holds none of them as a literal
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `contracts/evm/abi/Registry.json` and `contracts/evm/abi/IEntitlement.json` state, complete, the registry's and the entitlement interface's functions, events, and structures, and the concrete binds them through `alloy`'s `sol!`
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: adding a further chain concrete is its module, its variant in the member identifier, the selection, and each error union its own errors reach, its entry in the declared set, and its branch in the factory, the selection admission, the declaration lookup, the declared-selection builder, and the composition admission; no consumer changes, and no family surface names Base, an RPC, a gas price oracle, a block tag, or a contract call

  * `[ ]`   `role`
    * `[ ]`   Adapter: the chain family's concrete for Base, private beneath the family's `factory` module, the first concrete that implements the settlement and entitlement-state interfaces, and the first source file that requires them, the attempt context, the family's selection, declared set, consumer surface, fee description, and mock, so this node authors each of them in the `factory` module
    * `[ ]`   Owns the Base client over `alloy`, Base's tier mapping, the registry view calls, the fee estimate, the settlement event and calldata reader, and its own error types; reads the settings type, the declaration constant, and the family's member identifier, endpoint, and declaration types `chain/base/settings` authors

    * `[ ]`   Problem: configuration names a chain member by the name its declaration supplies and holds that member's settings as an untrusted table beneath the family's table, so the family holds one function that turns the identifier and the table into the selection the family's factory constructs from, and no configuration function, layer, or composition root names a concrete's guard, settings admission, declaration, or setting key

    * `[ ]`   Functional: a value the Base declaration fixes is refused, a settable value outside its declared bounds is refused, and a default its declaration states is taken, each by the member's own admission, so this function holds no bound, default, and setting name

* `[ ]`   `chain/admit_chain_composition` **The chain family's composition admission: a function from the member identifier its params carry and the declaration of the resolved chain forms its payload carries to success or to the family's `Incompatible` refusal, which names the forms identifier the member's declaration lists and the identifier the received forms declaration declares where the two disagree and names no concrete; it reads the member's declaration through the declaration lookup its deps carry, constructs nothing, and has no side effect; creates the `admit_chain_composition` module**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: whether a chain member can be composed with the forms concrete a caller resolved is determinate from declarations alone, so the family holds one function that compares the forms identifier the member's declaration lists with the identifier the resolved forms declare, before the factory constructs anything, and no caller restates a member's forms identifier from its own knowledge of the member
      * `[ ]`   Sources: the dependency map's `chain/admit_chain_composition` row and its conflict flags "Incompatible combinations are refused before construction", "the chain member against the resolved forms' identifier, admitted by `chain/admit_chain_composition`", and "The chain family's forms concrete precedes its chain concrete", "admitting a chain concrete through the composition admission only when its declaration lists the forms identifier the resolved forms carry"; the technical requirements' Chain family ownership list, "the composition admission `chain/admit_chain_composition`"; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope entry for `chain/admit_chain_composition` and its exit, "the chain factory refusing a forms concrete whose identifier the chain member's declaration does not list before constructing anything"; the product requirements' Resolved Positions on the adapter family form and on the contract suite as the on-chain half of the chain family; `families.md`, "Compatibility is admitted before construction and before effort", "Reading a declaration before construction", and "Ownership at the first consumer"; `composition.md`, "Nested calls build their inputs from what they receive (Rust)"
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the deps carry the declaration lookup as the handle `lookup_chain_declaration` typed `LookupChainDeclarationFn`, the params carry the member identifier `ChainMemberIdentifier`, and the payload carries the `ChainFormsDeclaration` of the resolved chain forms by reference
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the function reads the declaration of the member the identifier names through the handle and never from its caller, then compares the declaration's `forms_identifier` with the `identifier` of the received forms declaration
    * `[ ]`   Functional: when the two identifiers are equal the function returns `Ok(AdmitChainCompositionSuccessReturn)`, and when they differ it returns `Err(AdmitChainCompositionErrorReturn::Incompatible { forms })`, the field holding the identifier the member's declaration lists as `required` and the identifier the received declaration carries as `declared`
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the refusal carries identifiers only and names no concrete; the function constructs no adapter, reads no settings document, admits no selection, writes no file, and has no side effect
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: adding a further chain concrete adds no line to this node's files, because the function holds no arm per member and reads each member's requirement through the lookup; adding a further forms identifier adds its variant to `ChainFormsIdentifier` and the declarations that state it, and changes nothing here; the function holds no literal of an identifier, a name, a version, or an encoding

  * `[ ]`   `role`
    * `[ ]`   Adapter-ring family-owned function of the chain family: its own module beside `factory`, `evm_forms`, `create_chain_forms`, `base`, `admit_chain_selection`, and `lookup_chain_declaration`, ahead of the family's factory function, in the trusted form because its params and payload carry no untrusted data; it reads no clock, no environment variable, no network, and no filesystem
    * `[ ]`   Owns `AdmitChainCompositionDeps`, `AdmitChainCompositionParams`, `AdmitChainCompositionPayload`, `AdmitChainCompositionSuccessReturn`, `ChainFormsIdentifierDisagreement`, `AdmitChainCompositionErrorReturn`, `AdmitChainCompositionReturn`, `AdmitChainCompositionFn`, and the function; every other type the function names is a member's or the family's
    * `[ ]`   Does not construct an adapter, select a member, admit a member's settings, read a member's declaration other than through its handle, or construct forms; `chain/factory`, `chain/admit_chain_selection`, `chain/lookup_chain_declaration`, and `chain/create_chain_forms` do
